Plan Insights starts collapsed in a short window. The deadlock graph keeps its edge labels apart and sizes its cards to their text, and elapsed time shows without a zero day count.
- **The Job History tab's "Data starts" note** ([#8342]) - The time range picker on the Job History tab now names the oldest job history the store holds for the server or fleet on screen. It no longer names the start of the selected range. This applies to Lite and the Darling Viewer.
- **The Viewer shows grid cells whole, keeps its status bar to one line and to the tab on screen, and trims a long card title** ([#8332]) - Fleet Sweeps' Position and timestamp cells and Job History's Category cell (Lite too) grow to their text. The status bar trims to one line with the whole text as its tooltip, and it describes the tab on screen rather than the last read that finished. A Needs Attention row keeps a gap between the name and its reason. An Overview card trims a long name with a tooltip and keeps its status dot.
- **The web dashboard's opener policy behind a proxy, the PostgreSQL Waits intro, the High CPU notebook on PostgreSQL, the compose timeout answer, and the inventory read's cache** ([#8323]) - The dashboard sends the opener policy only when the configured public origin is trustworthy too, so a browser behind a proxy that rewrites Host stops logging an error on every page. The PostgreSQL Waits intro says Wait Trend fills on Amazon Aurora. The High CPU notebook carries the cells for the server's own engine. A notebook cell that the store stopped at its statement timeout says what it was reading and what to do, with the engine's text behind More detail. The FinOps Server Inventory read is kept in the read cache for five minutes.
- **query_store_hints no longer runs on a server whose every Query Store database is a readable secondary** ([#8361]) - The scheduler serves the slot without a run while the server's last known role leaves the collector nothing to read, and runs it again when the server is the primary. `get_collection_health` lists the collector as `not_collected` with the readable-secondary reason, for one server and across the fleet, instead of a run that needs a look.
- **An Overview chart whose read fails is cleared before it says so** ([#8368]) - A chart whose read fails after it drew data no longer keeps the old line under "read failed". A chart whose read fails twice in a row says so once. This applies to Lite and the Darling Viewer.
- **The Daily Health Calendar no longer takes 7 to 13 seconds on a server's first Overview after a service start** ([#8370]) - The service now builds each server's closed-day daily summary block two minutes after it starts, and again one minute after each UTC day boundary. It does one server at a time under the long-read limit, so the first visitor on any day finds it built. The new file setting `dailySummaryWarmerEnabled` turns the passes off.
- **A server tab stops its auto-refresh timer when its window closes** ([#8367]) - Lite's server tab and the Viewer's server tab now stop polling when the window they live in closes, instead of only when the tab is closed on its own. A tab switch keeps the timer.
- **Plan analysis tools give each top operator an execution-aware row estimate** ([#8377]) - Every `top_operators` entry from `analyze_plan_xml`, `analyze_query_plan`, `analyze_query_store_plan` and `analyze_procedure_plan` now carries `expected_rows` and `actual_executions` beside `estimated_rows` and `actual_rows`. An operator on the inner side of a Nested Loops join no longer reads as a misestimate when its per-execution estimate is compared with its total rows. Both are null on an estimated plan. The tool guide names the three row fields.
- **The plan viewer prints a fractional estimated execution count with its decimal** ([#8380]) - The Properties panel's Est. Executions keeps one decimal when the optimizer's rebind and rewind estimates give it one. So 10 rows per execution over 11.5 executions now reads 11.5 and 115.0 instead of 12 and 115.0. A whole count still prints with no decimal.
- **The scan rules report a plan that carries no row figure** ([#8381]) - Bare Scan applies its 10,000 row floor to an estimated plan only when the plan says how big the table is. Scan With Predicate applies its floor to an actual plan's rows read only when the plan recorded them, and uses the table's size times the scan's executions when it did not. A plan from a build that writes neither figure now gets its findings back instead of reading as a zero-row scan.
- **The Viewer's status bar no longer says a fleet tab is refreshed after its read failed** ([#8379]) - Each fleet tab (Alert History, Job History, Database Protection, Agent Alerts, Fleet Sweeps, Identity Capacity, Availability Groups, FinOps) now writes its own refreshed or failed line. The main window no longer writes a refreshed stamp over it.
- **The plan viewer's Wait Stats card fills a wide window instead of scrolling the Plan Insights strip** ([#8384]) - In a wide window the Wait Stats rows now trim with an ellipsis inside the card and the strip has nothing to scroll. In a narrow window the strip still scrolls to every card.
- **The plan viewer's Parameters card, warning headers and wait tooltips read with plain punctuation** ([#8387]) - The parameter notes, the sniffed-runtime tooltip, a warning header's benefit suffix, a wait row's category tooltip and the origin link's tooltip use a colon or a comma where they showed an em dash. This matches the rest of the analysis text. The Wait Stats header and a missing index row's Impact lead keep their dash as a separator between a name and a figure.
- **Alert History shows a long alert type whole instead of cutting it at the column's edge** ([#8392]) - The Alert Type column in Lite and the Darling Viewer grows to the longest alert type on screen, with its previous width as the minimum. Names such as Store Checkpointer Pressure now read in full.
- **Settings and mute-rule dialogs read evenly in Lite and the Darling Viewer** ([#8391]) - The Alert Muting card heading matches the other tabs' headings. The Analysis description sits under its heading like the others. The empty Manage Mute Rules message keeps to one line, and the Database State Overrides button names the Last Known State column it copies from.
- **Short time windows draw their strips by the minute, mute rule boxes fill the dialog, loading charts say so, narrow grid cells end with an ellipsis, Escape closes the toolbar overflow, and Settings notices use the app's own window** ([#8393]) - In Lite and the Darling Viewer the Deadlocks, Top Queries, Top Procedures and Query Store strips now reach the end of a Past hour window instead of stopping at the last whole hour. A Create Mute Rule box whose row partner is hidden spans the dialog. An Overview chart says it is loading until its first read returns. A grid cell too narrow for its text shows an ellipsis in every theme. The server toolbar's "..." overflow closes on Escape after a mouse click opened it. The Settings window's routine confirmations, such as Settings saved and test sent, appear in the app's own themed window instead of a native message box.
- **A BAD_ACTOR finding's per-execution CPU and reads now match its drill-down** ([#8403]) - The finding now sums every row of the window, including rows whose execution count is zero while CPU and reads moved. These are the same rows its drill-down sums and the top CPU queries already count in total CPU. So the finding's headline average and the figures beside it describe the same work.
- **query_store_hints stays off a readable standby whose Query Store data is frozen and whose role is not an Availability Group role** ([#8402]) - With the `query_store_health` collector disabled, a frozen all-secondary Query Store snapshot now gates the collector unless the newest `ag_replica_role` reads PRIMARY or SECONDARY. The Query Store reads, `get_collection_health` and the scheduler reach the same verdict from the same facts.
- **get_query_store_hints counts hints_returned as the page it lists** ([#8407]) - A call with a limit now answers `hints_returned` as the number of hints on that page, not the size of the whole read behind it. `total_hints` and `read_capped` still describe the whole set.
- **get_table_index_sizes names the snapshot each growth baseline was read from, and chooses it the way the database baselines are chosen** ([#8406]) - The history block carries `baseline_7d_snapshot` and `baseline_30d_snapshot`, so two equal growth figures can be seen to come from two snapshots. Each baseline is the snapshot nearest 7 or 30 days ago within a day, the same rule and tolerance that `get_storage_growth` uses. A missed daily run at the mark keeps the figure. A figure stands only when its span to the latest snapshot is 6 to 8 or 29 to 31 days to the nearest day. The reply says which baseline was out of reach and why.
- **find_queries_by_text returns the matches it found when a plan-cache search runs out of its budget** ([#8408]) - One server's plan-cache text is now searched newest first, in spans of the raw window, inside the same 4 second budget. Each text is judged once per search. A search that stops early returns the newest matches and says how much of the window it searched, instead of returning nothing.
- **MCP read-tool notes agree with their counts, lose their em dashes, and a sweep ships its worklist once** ([#8405]) - `get_collection_health` says "1 needs a look" and names the rows listed as not collected on the server. The `alert_read_health`, bucketed-trend and collector `last_error` texts use a colon or a comma instead of an em dash. `get_sweep_reports` carries the newest sweep's carried items and critical servers once each.
- **The Darling Viewer's About window describes what Darling monitors** ([#8412]) - The heading reads Performance Monitor Darling, as the title bar does. The description says the viewer shows dashboards, recommendations and alerts across every monitored SQL Server and PostgreSQL server, instead of calling the product a SQL Server tool.
- **A store whose database carries the TimescaleDB extension row purges its plain raw tables** ([#8422]) - The daily retention sweep leaves `query_stats`, `procedure_stats` and `query_store_stats` to the gated raw purge only where they are hypertables. A plain raw table in a database that holds the `timescaledb` extension row, such as one made from a template that carries it, is now purged at its horizon like any plain table. This holds whichever way the service's TimescaleDB flag reads. The procedure totals age out with it.
- **FinOps day tables read in the selected time display, and long cells trim** ([#8389]) - Lite's 7-Day Provisioning Trend and Memory Grant Efficiency now count their days in the selected time display instead of UTC. In both apps the Contention Events counter charts fill their cards and Server Inventory shows UNDER PROVISIONED whole. Database Sizes, Job History and the wait type picker trim long names with an ellipsis and a tooltip, and the empty Locking & Contention grid shows no scroll bar.
- **The Viewer's FinOps day tables read in the selected time display** ([#8443]) - The 7-Day Provisioning Trend and Memory Grant Efficiency now count their days in the selected time display instead of UTC, so a row's day matches the hours on the charts beside it. The MCP FinOps tools keep UTC days.
- **The plan viewer resets its panels when a plan is pasted over another** ([#8446]) - Pasting a plan into a viewer that already shows one closes the previous plan's Statements and Properties panels. It also drops its statement list and the button that reopens it, so the new plan appears alone. Text that does not parse shows the empty state alone.
- **The plan viewer centres an operator it goes to, and rounds the zoom percentage** ([#8449]) - Going to an operator from a warning's link now puts the operator itself in the middle of the view instead of its top-left corner. The zoom percentage is the nearest whole number instead of a truncated one.
- **The FinOps Optimization tab's cold read is shorter and is kept per server** ([#8453]) - The optimization read runs its two 24-hour raw scans together and its other three sections one at a time around them. The answer for a server is kept for five minutes from the moment it is stored, so a second visit or the tab's own refresh does not pay the read again.
- **A Cleared alert's triage header no longer prints 0.0 against 0.0** ([#8454]) - The alert notebook header and the triage page's alert card show the dashes that Alert History shows for a resolution row's stored zeros. They leave the value line out when neither side was measured.
- **The standalone plan viewer keeps a reloaded tab's label and says when Ctrl+V cannot read the clipboard** ([#8459]) - Pasting or dropping a plan into a tab that already holds one keeps the plain label instead of adding a counter. Ctrl+V shows the same notice as the Paste XML button when another app holds the clipboard.
- **The plan viewer names a statement's parameterization type** ([#8460]) - The Properties panel's Parameterization Type row reads User, Simple or Forced instead of the showplan code. A 0, which the engine also writes for a parameterized statement when Query Store is not READ_WRITE, reads "Not recorded". It comes with a hint when the statement's parameters are numbered, which means simple or forced parameterization or a prepared statement. A plan without the attribute has no row. A code outside the list still shows as its number.
- **The daily summary block the service builds before a server's first visitor is now the one the Overview reads** ([#8451]) - The closed-day block is now keyed by the store a connection reaches rather than by the connection pool. So the block built after a service start serves the first Overview visit through the web dashboard, and an MCP call for the same 31-day range, instead of each paying the cold read.
- **The Queries tab's small reads and the page's own status reads no longer wait behind its Query Store reads** ([#8452]) - The web dashboard now sends at most two of the tab's four heavy Query Store reads at once and every other read straight away. The status bar, the store size, the alert badge and the tab's small panels answer in their own time instead of queueing behind reads that take seconds.
- **The plan viewer's Parameters card shows a cut value in full on hover** ([#8455]) - A Compiled or Runtime value that the column trims now carries its whole text as a tooltip, at any length. A sniffed runtime value's tooltip gives the value first and the sniffing note after it.
- **The plan viewer shows Operator Details for every operator that has one** ([#8456]) - An operator whose only details were a FORCESEEK column count, Halloween protection, a partition id or one of sixteen other rows now gets its Operator Details section, and the rows copy with "Copy all properties".
- **The plan viewer's Parameters card no longer flags names inside literals and comments as variables** ([#8457]) - An @name inside a string literal, a comment, a quoted identifier or a bracketed name is text. The card and the repro script now report only variables in code.
- **Collapsing an operator's Warnings section in the plan viewer hides its warnings** ([#8458]) - The warnings now sit inside the section, so its header collapses and expands them as the Plan Warnings header already does.
- **The plan viewer counts a statement's warnings one way** ([#8447]) - The root operator's badge, its tooltip and the Statements grid all count the statement-level warnings and every operator's warnings, at every severity. A statement whose findings are all statement-level now shows them on the root.
- **A whole-hours time range hint names the rounded-up length** ([#8448]) - A FinOps picker that takes whole hours now tells a typed 25.5h to try 26h instead of 1d, so following the hint never shortens the range.
- **An agent's mute rule without a claim is held to the same scope as one under a claim** ([#8473]) - Without `agent_action`, a mute now needs one server and a metric that is not one of the service's own alerts. A mute that breaks this is refused with the same reason words the claim path uses. A digest can still name any server or metric. The hourly cap counts each agent's own writes. A write before the service publishes its store owner connection is refused instead of failing.
- **The Block Chain window's empty-state text reads the same in Lite and the Darling viewer** ([#8467]) - Both apps now build the window's model through one shared projection. The text that says no blocking was found uses a comma where the viewer had a dash.
- **Darling's get_cpu_utilization description says sample_time is UTC** ([#8469]) - Lite's twin stamps on the monitored server's clock and said so, while Darling stamps in UTC and said nothing. An agent that read both had no way to tell the two clocks apart from the tool text. Darling's description now says UTC.
- **The FinOps Object Growth heatmap reads its days in the selected time display** ([#8450]) - The Storage Growth drill's heatmap columns now count their days in the selected time display instead of UTC, in Lite and the Viewer, so a column's day matches the day tables beside it. The MCP storage growth tool keeps UTC days.
- **An offline Lite server card hides its memory figure and says when the server was last collected** ([#8465]) - Every measurement on an offline card now reads "--", and the CPU row says "last collected N ago", as the Darling card does. The figures stay on the card item for the alert engine.
- **Lite and Darling share one MCP token floor, and Lite refuses a weak replacement token** ([#8462]) - The rule that a token needs at least 16 characters and 6 different ones before the write and investigation tools are served is now defined once for both apps. Lite's token swap in Settings refuses a token under that floor and keeps the old one. Each app's `allowTokenlessLoopback` description now says what the setting does there.
- **The deadlock graph and the blocking chain round their zoom percentage** ([#8491]) - Both controls show the nearest whole percent, as the plan viewer does, so three Zoom Out steps read 55% instead of 54%.
- **A saved Custom View's alert-history table no longer prints 0.00 for a Cleared alert** ([#8492]) - A saved view's Value and Threshold columns show the dash that Alert History shows for a resolution row's stored zeros. A measured row keeps its numbers.
- **FinOps Server Inventory shows Edition, Version and Host OS whole** ([#8474]) - In the Viewer and in Lite the three text columns size to their longest value. The Hardware Note column is shown only while a server carries a note, so the grid needs less horizontal scroll.
- **The MCP server instructions match each app's investigation gate** ([#8463]) - Lite's instructions state the token rule the server enforces for the tools that claim and report, and count them from the policy. Darling's instructions name every investigation tool, including `post_investigation_message`. Both lists follow the policy's set from now on.
- **The FinOps day tables keep a sample stamped after the read took its clock** ([#8503]) - The 7-Day Provisioning Trend and Memory Grant Efficiency tables run their days through the window's newest sample, in Lite and the Viewer. A sample that the store's host wrote after the Viewer's clock, or that the collector stamped after Lite took its clock, now lands in the row its day names instead of leaving the table. MCP day labels stay UTC.
- **Apps & features shows the Lite and Viewer names and their publisher** ([#8414]) - The Velopack installers now list the apps as Performance Monitor Lite and Performance Monitor Darling Viewer, with Darling Data, LLC as the publisher. Before, Apps & features showed the package id for both.
- **Plan analysis words say "1 write", "1 lookup" and "1 wait type"** ([#8517]) - The exchange spill warning reads "1 write to TempDB". The Key Lookup grade reads "over 1 lookup". The Lazy Spool message reads "1 rewind (cache hit)". The wait stats header reads "across 1 wait type".
- **Every MCP time-series tool says which clock its stamps are on** ([#8502], [#8524]) - Both apps' trend, snapshot and daily-summary reads now say their stamps are UTC, not the monitored server's clock. They point at get_server_properties for the server's offset. Only get_cpu_utilization said so before, so an agent reading a Lite answer beside a Darling one had to guess.
- **Alert toasts stay readable and follow the theme** ([#8475]) - A long alert message now scrolls inside the toast instead of growing it past the screen. A toast on screen takes the new colors when the theme changes. A theme change no longer leaves a toast that cannot close while the next toast opens over it. This covers both the Viewer and Lite.
- **Counts and the figures beside them read in one convention** ([#8522]) - These now write every number the same way on every desktop: the purge summary, the wait sampling summary, the dormant-database finding, the predicate statistics and index bloat notes, and the store repair's failure line. A count no longer reads as a decimal next to a figure in the local convention.
- **Darling's Last Collect stamp and sidebar freshness dot count only successful collections** ([#8464]) - A failed collection attempt no longer moves the Overview card's Last Collect, the sidebar dot, list_servers or get_server_summary forward. A server Darling cannot read from no longer reads fresh. A server whose collectors run and all fail reads Offline on every surface in both apps, and its offline note points at the failing collectors. A server that is not collected yet still reads as awaiting its first collection. Every surface agrees with get_collection_health on whether the service can connect. A new index keeps the newest-success read cheap for a server whose every collector fails. Both apps now share one definition of what counts as a collection.
- **A recovery goes to the channel its firing went to** ([#8526]) - "Server Restored" and "AG Replica Reconnected" are routed at the severity their firing was delivered at, per replica for an availability group, so with a Critical-only route and a Warning-only route for one family the recovery follows its firing to the same route, and its PagerDuty resolve closes the incident on that route's key.
- **No false Capture Down after a failover or restart the service recovers from** ([#8530]) - A capture collector that finds its session stopped starts it and reads again before it records anything, a session the service itself restarted is not reported down until its collector has read it again, and the alert's detail says what the service saw (a stopped session it could not start, with the server's words, one it started that stopped again, or a check that did not complete) instead of claiming a create failed. A Capture Down that ends because the session was refused for permission or its collector was turned off closes as "Capture Cleared", not "Capture Restored".
- **The FinOps Object Growth Heatmap is readable in a small window, and the Server Inventory says what it has not collected** ([#8543]) - The heatmap keeps a row for every object and scrolls with its grid, its labels no longer overlap, and a day with no data shows as an empty column. Server Inventory shows "Not collected" in place of an empty Status or a dash for Health, Lite's Last Updated follows the selected time display, and Lite's Index Analysis lists databases from the newest size snapshot of the last seven days. The 7-day provisioning trend lists the six whole days before today and today so far, and a time shown on a server's own clock names its offset.
- **Daily Summary keeps its calendar readable after a day is clicked** ([#8544]) - In Lite and the Viewer the month calendar shrank to rows about 20 px tall when the day detail opened in a small window. The calendar now scrolls to the day detail and the day cells stay readable. A day with one deadlock or one blocking event reads "<0.1/hr" where it read "0.0/hr", and the Viewer's Overview cards, the web fleet card, the PostgreSQL blocking reason and the analysis advice follow the same rule.
- **Collection Health says which days its connection failures come from** ([#8545]) - The Collection Cycles card in Lite and the Viewer captioned both of its lines "in the last 24 hours" while the connection-failure line is read from the grid's seven days, so an older failure looked out of place. The caption now covers the skipped slots alone and the connection-failure line says "in the last 7 days".
- **Lite's status bar counts the seconds since the last collection** ([#8545]) - "Collection: Ns ago" was repainted every 30 seconds against a one-minute collection cycle, so an idle window kept showing the same two numbers and could show Running after a cycle had ended. The label now ticks every second and updates the moment a cycle starts or ends.
- **A Darling upgrade no longer refuses over a PostgreSQL backend that has just exited** ([#8546]) - `upgrade-darling.ps1` decides from the path it read when it listed each process, so a connection that ends while it checks what is running from the install folder no longer stops the upgrade, and its refusal gives that path for every process it names. The same check now refuses to run from a 32-bit PowerShell on 64-bit Windows or when the service is not stopped and its own process cannot be found, and it sees a process under the folder the service is registered at when `-InstallRoot` spells that folder another way or the process was started by a short path.
- **Grids draw quickly when their rows arrive at an empty grid, and the deadlock graph and blocking chain open fitted in the middle of their window** ([#8556]) - A grid that was empty and wider than its window built every row when its rows arrived, which made Blocked Process Reports, Memory Broker and Job History take several seconds to draw, and slowed Top Queries by Duration and Query Store by Duration, in Lite and the Darling Viewer. It now builds only the rows in view, and the Memory Broker grid reads only the events it can show. The deadlock graph and the blocking chain are drawn in the middle of their viewer instead of the top-left corner, and one larger than its viewer opens zoomed out to fit.
- **Servers with a negative id work on the web dashboard, in the MCP tag tools and in external alert muting** ([#8557]) - A server whose id is negative, about half of all servers, got no alert badge on the web dashboard, its favourite star did not toggle, its Databases filter was refused with a message that the set was too large, and a note could not be written for it. It could not be tagged or untagged, on the Manage Tags page or with `assign_server_tag` and `unassign_server_tag`, and a mute rule keyed on its id did not silence the alerts an external source sent for it. An acknowledged badge and a Databases filter are now kept across a page load for every server, so a filter chosen before the upgrade that the page had dropped shows again until it is cleared with All databases. A server whose id is exactly -1 is no longer taken for "no server": an investigating agent on it can no longer delete or disable a mute rule made for every server, and a read with no server no longer uses its collector schedule.
- **Remediation SQL gives no force statement for a plan the store blocks** ([#8558]) - A plan regression finding's copy-paste remediation no longer includes `sp_query_store_force_plan` for a plan automatic plan correction owns, withdrew or resolved differently, a plan already forced, or a plan whose forcing already failed. Such a target is named in comments with its blockers, and when the forcing state could not be read the text says no target was checked. This covers the MCP analysis tools, the web findings page, the Viewer's and Lite's recommendation cards and finding notifications. A parameter-sensitive or secondary-replica target keeps its statement under its caution.
- **Check boxes show in every theme, long chart legend entries are no longer cut, and several texts stay readable on the light themes** ([#8559]) - A check box and a radio button are outlined like the controls beside them, with a readable mark on Light and a paler outline when disabled. A legend entry wider than its chart, such as the restart marker's sentence on a Performance Trends chart in a small window or a long database name, wraps onto more lines or ends in an ellipsis instead of being cut mid-word, and the hover over a shortened entry shows its whole text. The plan viewer's warning text, the Darling Viewer's "Access: read-only" and the FinOps locking detail rows follow the theme. The history windows theme their chart legend and read "1 sample" for one sample.
- **The plan viewer starts each plan at 100%, keeps room for the plan beside its panels, and stops cutting Plan Insights text** ([#8560]) - A plan pasted over another is drawn at 100% instead of the previous plan's zoom, and with the Statements and Properties panels both open the panels narrow so the plan keeps 240 px of the viewer, with one panel showing at a time where the viewer is too narrow for both. In Plan Insights the Server Context sentence wraps and a long parameter name is shortened so the Compiled and Runtime columns stay readable, and the zoom, Fit and Minimap buttons are disabled when no plan is drawn.
- **Lite's Query Store grid opens a plan from its View button** ([#8560]) - The inline View button was disabled on every row. It now fetches the row's plan from the monitored server's Query Store by plan id, as the right-click menu does, shows it on the Plan Viewer tab, and says why when the fetch fails.
- **Query Store collection stands down on a read replica whose role reads PRIMARY** ([#8561]) - The Query Store hints collector no longer runs every slot on the forwarder of a distributed availability group, where every database's Query Store is a secondary's, and its run records that so the answer outlasts the health capture. The Query Store reads name the secondary replica as the reason for READ_ONLY instead of the storage cap, and a stale Query Store health or automatic-tuning capture says when its collector is disabled, and otherwise names the cadence that collector is scheduled at. The Query Store panel in the Viewer and in Lite gives the same reasons over an empty window instead of only saying the window has no data.
- **Collection keeps its slots after a Darling restart** ([#8562]) - Scheduled analysis passes run inside collection slots, and after a restart every server's first pass came due together and held most of them while collection waited and skipped minutes. Passes now hold at most half the slots and give way for up to five minutes to collection that has waited 30 seconds or more for a slot, and a late skipped-collection row names the wait that cost its slots instead of saying no cause was measured.
- **Alert notebooks read correctly for Capture Restored, External alerts, CPU Resolved and report rows** ([#8592]) - The Capture Restored row said "No resolution recorded" although it is the resolution of the Capture Down before it, and Capture Down never said "Resolved at". Both now match, as do Capture Cleared and Collection Resumed with their firings, and an External alert now finds its own resolution. An External alert whose source is not a monitored server no longer opens with "Could not resolve server" and two failed cells: it keeps its own card with no per-server cells. The CPU Resolved notebook on a SQL Server server no longer has an Instance CPU (Performance Insights) cell, and one on a PostgreSQL server no longer has the SQL Server CPU reads. The report rows (Fleet Sweep Rollup, Collector Cost Digest, Analysis Singles Digest) now show only "Reports have no notebook", with no Alert notebook header, buttons or empty-cells line.
- **Notebook notes draw tables and quotes, and several small web texts read cleanly** ([#8592]) - A notebook markdown cell showed a pipe table as one paragraph of pipes and a "> " line with the ">" in front. Tables (with column alignment) and block quotes now render, and HTML inside a cell still shows as text. An Investigations evidence item whose key_numbers holds JSON is drawn as that object instead of an escaped string, in the web page and in the Viewer. FinOps on a store with only PostgreSQL servers says "Not collected for PostgreSQL" on Server Inventory (web and Viewer) instead of "yet", and the two Utilization strips carry their panel titles. The plan-capture "More detail" text says "precondition" or "preconditions" to match the count. The Fleet Sweeps settle-window note, the Vacuum xmin note and the Custom Views empty state lose their em dashes.
- **Four MCP replies stop misleading callers** ([#8593]) - `get_collection_log` showed `truncated: true` beside a `runs_total` equal to the page size, so a caller could not tell how many runs matched. `runs_total` is now the count of matching runs (Darling and Lite, per-server and fleet forms). `get_identity_capacity` took `soonest_exhaustion_days` from the one column with a projection and skipped a full column. It now reports 0 when a column has no values left and adds `columns_without_projection` with a note. `get_long_query_completions` answered `empty` for a collector that never ran. It now answers `not_collected` with the step to turn it on (Darling and Lite), or when the collector is already enabled and has not had its first pass, with a note to read again after it does. The `get_read_latency` note lost a stray space before a colon and a missing "is", and its long description gained the same missing "is".
- **MCP replies: rounded numbers, no em dashes, clearer notes and advice that matches the data** ([#8595]) - Several reads showed float noise such as 48.06999999999999 or 257.70384995064165. Table and database sizes, daily wait seconds and wait-trend rates are now rounded where the reply is built. Precondition, routing, CPU attribution, sweep and plan-force messages no longer use em dashes, and the plan-force warning no longer shows a tracker number or "the bot". The deadlock ring buffer note reads as two sentences. The get_pg_plans precondition message counts the unmet items and lists them in the order to fix them. The plan_text_setting detail says so when auto_explain is not loaded. get_pg_index_usage picks its text from the index's own flags and scans. The bad-actor text no longer calls a one-second query cheap, and the PostgreSQL anomaly text no longer points at a card the reply may not hold. Blocking capture counts and block accesses carry thousands separators.
- **Settings > Apps shows which nightly of the Darling Viewer is installed** ([#8596]) - A Setup.exe install showed version 3.12.0 for every nightly. The Viewer and Lite now write their full installed version to that entry each time they start, so it matches the About window.
- **The FinOps Optimization tab and the `get_finops` optimization view no longer scan a day of raw query stats on a first visit** ([#8597]) - the most-expensive-queries read summed every raw row of the 24 hour window, which on a large store with cold buffers was slow and could run out of time. It now takes the whole hours from the hourly rollup and reads raw only for the partial first hour and the newest hours, so a first visit touches a few percent of the pages. The CPU ranking and sums are unchanged. Executions and reads now include samples that used no CPU, and statements are grouped by query hash instead of text, so a few rows can merge or split. Stores without the rollup, or without the per-hash text index, read as before.
- **Closing a plan tab, a plan window or a server tab gives its memory back** ([#8598], [#8680]) - Lite and the Viewer kept every closed plan viewer and server tab in memory until the app exited, with the plan or the rows it showed. They are now released when they close. A grid still showing "Loading..." held on to the closed server tab and all of its charts, and that is released too. Each server tab that was opened, looked through and closed had kept 500 to 700 MB until Lite was restarted, and its memory now comes back within about a minute. The Darling Viewer uses the same code and gets the same fix.
- **The oversized-plan backlog no longer grows without bound, and its read no longer scans dropped rows** ([#8599], [#8696]) - Each sweep pass now drops, without a fetch, the waiting rows it would never reach: earlier sightings of a query that has since been sighted under a new plan handle, and the stalest rows past 28,800 waiting on one server. A dropped row is deleted at once and counted per server, collector and hour in a new table, `collect.oversized_plan_backlog_drops` (store migration V264, a new table only, no existing row is moved or deleted). get_oversized_plan_backlog reports dropped rows apart from pending, captured and expired, and no longer counts them as drain. The last-day dropped and added counts are accurate to the hour, and a plan that is dropped, seen again and dropped again counts twice. Size figures and the oldest sighting cover the rows the store still holds, not the dropped ones. Rows an older build held age out as before and are counted with the new counts while they last. A build older than V264 does not read the counts, so its dropped figures read low for a day after a rollback.
- **get_oversized_plan_backlog answers a fleet call faster** ([#8675]) - The median size per server and per collector is now read through two new indexes (store migration V260) instead of sorting every row, and the collector census counts its servers without sorting every row. On a test store of 3,000,000 backlog rows a fleet call took about 3 seconds instead of 12 to 22. The numbers are unchanged.
- **analyze_server and get_analysis_facts no longer slow down with the number of database files and kept trace events** ([#8600]) - Two statements in the analysis fact reads grew with the size of the monitored server and not with the window. The database-size check compared every file of two older snapshots with every file of the newest one, 16 million comparisons and 2.1 seconds on a test store with 4,000 files, and now hashes the file names once (25 ms). The sp_configure trace read scanned every default-trace event the server had kept for 30 days and now reads only the events collected near the span it asks about (30,629 buffers down to 1,355 on a test store of 1.5 million events). The facts and their windows are unchanged.
- **get_table_index_sizes and get_store_metrics read less on a first call** ([#8600]) - get_table_index_sizes found its snapshot times by aggregating every index row of the server's history, and get_store_metrics walked every retained hourly sweep to find the newest exactly sized one. Each now finds those rows with a few index probes. The replies are unchanged.
- **The FinOps Server Inventory reads less on a first call** ([#8600], [#8678]) - The statement behind the get_finops_inventory tool, the Viewer inventory grid and the web Server Inventory page read the newest database size snapshot per server with a join that walked every chunk of the size history twice, so a first call grew with retention. It now probes each server's newest snapshot through its index. The 24 hour CPU window is now read on its own, in file order, and sorted in memory, instead of grouped through the server and time index, which visited the table's pages server by server in a scattered order, so the statement touches about 40 percent fewer pages and reads them in sequence. The figures shown are unchanged.
- **Five small Viewer defects are fixed** ([#8603]) - The Active Queries plan tab was titled with an em dash and now reads "Estimated Plan - Session N" or "Live Plan - Session N". The Active Queries header read "Currently Running Queries" over stored captures and now reads "Captured queries", in the Viewer and in Lite. A stale row on Configuration > Query Store now shows its age after the Collected time once it is older than two cycles of its collector, in the Viewer and in Lite. Recommendations "Generate now" on a read-only connection now says why it is disabled. Database Protection "Captured", Recommendations "Last analyzed", Agent Alerts "Captured" and the Identity Capacity "Last scanned" column now read on the same clock as the page's refresh stamp, UTC in Server Time mode, instead of showing the server's offset.
- **MCP reads fit the 32,768-byte response budget and say when they were cut for it** ([#8605], [#8668], [#8689]) - Alert history, the store log, top queries, forced plans, PostgreSQL session states and server config, and the fleet cards now end at the last whole row that fits 32,768 bytes, flag the cut with how many rows were left out, and page to the rest. Four trend reads use wider default points so a default answer fits. get_table_index_sizes answered over the budget without saying so on a server with 100 or more tables. It now ends at the last whole table that fits, sets budget_truncated and budget_note, and takes a new offset parameter with next_offset to read on to every table. get_daily_summary_range with a wide days_back (more than about 70 days) returned a reply over the budget with no sign of it. It now keeps the newest whole days that fit, sets budget_truncated, and gives in budget_note the as_of and days_back that read the older days. A 30-day reply is unchanged. Darling and Lite both do this.
- **A server's page in the web dashboard paints sooner on a first visit** ([#8606], [#8687], [#8701]) - The page sent the daily summary, the baseline read and the three 24 hour trends together, and on a server nobody had opened they took several seconds each and held most of the browser's connections. They now go two at a time, charts first, and the longest read (the Daily Summary and Daily Health Calendar) goes first. The daily summary warm-up also looks again every minute, not every 15, while a start-up repair runs, and logs one line saying why it is waiting. Every panel on a server's page also used to wait for the fleet roll-up, because that answer says which tab set (SQL Server or PostgreSQL) the server has. The page now starts the SQL Server Overview reads while the roll-up is still on its way, and keeps those panels when the answer agrees. The page still waits for the roll-up for a server this browser has seen as PostgreSQL, for a tab only PostgreSQL servers have, and when a database filter is saved. A first visit to a PostgreSQL server starts the SQL Server reads and then replaces them. The browser now remembers which servers were PostgreSQL, as a hash of the server name and never the name itself.
- **Lite refuses the reserved agent ids and logs wrong-token refusals, and two Darling gaps close** ([#8612]) - Lite let an agent claim an investigation as `invalid` or `unclaimed`, the names its own refusal rows use, and it logged nothing when a request carried a wrong MCP token. Lite and Darling now also reserve `legacy-token`, the name a no-claim write through the legacy token is journaled under, so no claim or token name can pose as it. Lite refuses a claim under a reserved name as Darling does, and writes one rate-limited log line per run of refused tokens in each refusal state, never showing the token. Darling's start line for the investigation tools now lists every tool the arm serves, with the agent action tools as a count, where it named only two. Deleting a notification route over the web now needs the JSON content type, as the mute routes already did.
- **The MCP write-gate docs, tool descriptions and start logs now say what the gates do** ([#8613]) - The docs and sample files counted 13 agent action tools with 4 server actions when there are 16 with 7, named three server admin tools and two investigation tools when there are four and three, said every write needs `mcp.allowWrites` when a named `operator` token gets the writes with it off, and left out `mcp.allowAgentMuteRules`, the limits on mutes without a claim, the six-different-characters token floor and Lite's twelve write tools. They are corrected, and a new section lists the four intended differences between Lite and Darling. About 50 Darling tool and parameter descriptions said a write needs `mcp.allowWrites`, so a client could wrongly conclude it could not write. They now say "or a named operator token", and the `agent_action` parameter text says it is ignored when the write tools are served. The refusal message for an unserved agent action also names the named token roles. The instructions and start logs named three server admin tools when there are four. Lite's instructions said `mcp_allow_writes` was off when it was on but the tokenless opt-out was on or the token was under the floor, and now say which. Darling's instructions now say a named agent token or the unnamed token under `mcp.allowInvestigations` can mute without a claim, and give the limits (one server, a metric that is not a service alert, up to 7 days, 20 writes an hour, no repeat for 14 days). The CLI note and three start or refusal lines now also say the token floor needs 6 different characters. Only text changed.
- **The service log and the darling.json reference no longer say that `web.allowAdminWrites` off makes the dashboard list-only** ([#8614]) - The start line, the OIDC warning for a sign-in with no role mapping and the sample file's comment now name the Admin writes the setting turns off. They also say that a sign-in that can edit still adds and edits monitored servers and writes mute rules, dismissals and custom alert rules, and the warning says every signed-in user can do so.
- **The Viewer's Query Store by Duration slicer loads faster on stores with compressed history** ([#8617]) - On a store with a month of Query Store history, the slicer's read spent most of its time sorting every raw row twice, once per chunk and once more across the whole window, and wrote a 128 MB temp file on the way. It now sorts each chunk on a cheaper key, keeps the same newest copy of each interval, and totals the buckets with a hash aggregate. The bars are unchanged, and on a test store of 3.5 million rows the read took about 6.8 s instead of 14.5 s.
- **get_top_queries_by_cpu reads half the pages on a server's default 24-hour window** ([#8618]) - The top-queries statement built the wide row of its 25 winners by scanning the whole window a second time, so a first call on a busy server read every page twice. It now reads only the winners' rows through the existing query-hash index, with the same results. On a test store of 360,000 window rows it went from 73,100 pages and 1,018 ms to 37,700 pages and 663 ms.
- **get_forced_plans is faster on a first call and on stores with many databases** ([#8618], [#8641], [#8690]) - On a store whose table statistics were a day or two old, the 24 hour read took far too long because the planner thought the window held one row and chose nested loops that re-read it thousands of times. The read now plans without nested loops, in a short read-only transaction that leaves the connection settings alone. On a test store with stale statistics it dropped from 3 to 5 seconds to about 40 milliseconds. On stores that monitor a server with hundreds of databases, the count that tells whether a forced plan was ever missing from a capture read every capture marker of every database once per forced plan. It now reads the markers only for databases that have a forced plan in the window, and on a test store of 274 forces and 600 databases it went from about 22 seconds to about 0.35 seconds. The list also no longer sorts every capture row of the window twice to find each force's newest row and first sighting, and the per-database forced-plan count no longer sorts every capture row (1,110 ms down to 119 ms on 440,000 rows of a test store). Results are unchanged.
- **`get_query_fleet_summary` reads whole closed days from the daily rollup** ([#8641]) - A default 7-day window spent most of its time decompressing compressed hourly day-chunks to keep a few rows per hash. The statement now reads whole closed days older than the daily policy's 4-day window from the daily rollup, and only the edge days and the newest days from the hourly one. On a field-ratio test store (2,000 hashes per server per hour, compressed rollups) the default 7-day window reads about 1.7 times fewer data pages and runs about 1.8 times faster cold (312 ms to 177 ms), a 14-day window about 3.9 times fewer pages and 3.6 times faster, and a 30-day window about 7 times fewer pages and 6.4 times faster, with the same rows, order and first and last hours. A store without a complete daily rollup, or a window with fewer than 2 eligible whole days, keeps the old read.
- **The analysis plan fetch sorts far less** ([#8641]) - get_analysis_facts and the analysis drill-down ranked every window row with its plan content in the sort. The plan fetch now ranks thin rows and reads only the ten winning plans. On a test store the sort's spill to temp files dropped about 8 times, while the pages read stayed about the same. Lite's plan fetch (the advisory facts and their drill-down) ranks thin rows the same way. Results are unchanged.
- **get_query_store_clutter and its web and Viewer twins no longer read the plan-churn window one pass after the other** ([#8675]) - The per-plan figures and the count of distinct collection times are two statements run together, so a first call waits for the slower one, not for both. The numbers are unchanged.
- **MCP replies round the last long decimals, group the last long counts and drop the last tracker numbers** ([#8619], [#8676], [#8688]) - get_top_queries_by_cpu and get_top_procedures_by_cpu averages, the duration-trend rates and the daily-summary blocking rate no longer print 13 or more decimals, and a rare blocking rate keeps two significant digits instead of reading 0. analyze_server, get_analysis_facts, get_analysis_findings, get_sweep_reports and get_plan_force_precheck, which still wrote figures such as 9.531238439784213, now round them (9.53). Ages and counts in sentences carry thousands separators ("CHECKDB: Sales (2,196 days 6 hours old)", "the residual of 587,455 bytes"), and so do the trend and oversized plan notes (1,500 and 524,288). Replies from the alert settings, collection health, deadlock, store statement statistics, oversized plan and trend reads no longer show a tracker number such as #3712. The confidence_basis of every analysis finding no longer opens with "corroboration (#3538)", and the Query Store clutter note, the slow-read and read-latency guides, the PostgreSQL logging audit and other tool descriptions no longer name tracker numbers or the V137 rung. get_alert_settings no longer names an internal issue number, get_pg_session_states says "1 transaction's", and a story's "Behind it" sentence for a spilling query gives that query's own page count, not the window total. Lite gets the same fixes. New tests call every MCP read on a seeded store, in both Darling and Lite, and fail when a reply holds an em dash, a tracker number or a count of four or more digits without separators.
- **analyze_server returns every finding, trimming the ones that do not fit the response budget** ([#8579]) - One large finding used to push out every smaller finding behind it, so a reply of about 8 KB could say only 1 of 7 findings fit a 32 KB budget. The reply now keeps packing in severity order. A finding that does not fit the room left is trimmed to fit it (drill-down rows set to null first, then the remediation commands together with their risk disclosure, with a trimmed field naming what was cut) instead of being skipped, so a run anchored with as_of still shows every finding. A remediation command is never returned without its risk disclosure. A finding that does not fit even fully trimmed is left out and listed in the new findings_left_out field by category and headline, and truncation_note says how many were trimmed or left out and where to read the cut parts. Lite and Darling both changed.
- **The PostgreSQL "connection count high" starter alert now sees connections on every capture** ([#8580]) - The starter read the session total from the session table, which stores a row only while a session holds an old transaction, so the rule read no data for much of the day. It now reads a new "Connected backends" measure, which adds the per-database backend counts within each capture and is present on every capture. The key, the 200 and 500 thresholds and their meaning are unchanged. Rules already saved from the old starter are not rewritten and keep reading the session total. The "Total sessions" measure stays, and its note now says it exists only on captures that stored a session row.
- **compare_analysis rows say which reading they were banded on, and a negligible graded move gets no verdict** ([#8581]) - A connection-saturation row was banded on peak sessions but showed only the fraction, so a fraction that fell could read worse with no session count on the row. Rows banded on a reading other than the one they show (connection saturation and WAL volume shift) now carry both windows' banded readings with their metric and unit. A fact the scorer grades at severity 0 in both windows also earned worse or better under the baseline band and was counted in the family totals. It now gets no verdict and is not counted, and keeps delta_sigma and beyond_anomaly_cutoff. Facts the scorer never grades, such as session count and transaction rate, keep their baseline verdict.
- **A read-only Viewer no longer offers Mute and Dismiss in the Alert History row menu, or Remove and Silence in the server menu** ([#8585]) - On a Viewer connected with the read-only role, the Alert History toolbar disabled Dismiss, but right-clicking a row still offered Mute This Alert, Mute Similar Alerts and Dismiss Selected, and Mute opened a Create Mute Rule dialog that could not be saved. Those three items are now disabled with a tooltip that says why, and so are Remove, Silence This Server and Unsilence in the sidebar server menu. The Alert History tooltip on the two Dismiss buttons now also mentions muting.
- **Investigations shows each server under one name, and a picked request now comes into view** ([#8586]) - The web Investigations list drew the name a request was opened with, so one server could appear under its display name and its full host name, and the long names made the table wider than a 1280 px window. The Server column now shows the registry display name (the opened-as name is in the cell's title and the request pane), new requests are stored under the display name, and the Lease until and Route columns sit behind a "Lease and route" toggle so the table fits. Picking a request scrolls its pane into view and moves focus to its heading. The desktop Viewer's Requests tab shows the display name too. The web sidebar no longer pushes the server list off a short window: the page links scroll inside their own block with a button to fold them away (remembered per browser), the Servers heading, search box and Group by tag stay pinned, and the server list scrolls on its own.
- **The Query Store duration trend no longer reads low on its newest point** ([#8587]) - The newest point is the Query Store interval that is still open, and it was rated over its whole hour although only part of the hour had run, so it showed a drop that was not in the workload. It is now rated over the time it has covered, and that point carries partial: true and covered_seconds. An open interval that has run under 60 seconds has null rates and counts in unrated_points. Closed intervals are unchanged. Lite and the Darling MCP tool both change.
- **The web sidebar's alert badges no longer download 1,000 alert rows on every page load and every minute, and no longer undercount on a busy store** ([#8588]) - The badges were counted from the newest 1,000 rows of alert history, about 1.8 MB per read on a busy store, and a window with more than 1,000 rows lost its oldest actionable alerts. The page now asks for a small per-server summary (the count of alerts that are not muted, dismissed or resolutions in the last 24 hours, whether any is critical, and the newest alert time), grouped in the store with no row cap. A scoped token or access group still sees only the servers it is allowed to see.
- **Viewer text and format fixes on PostgreSQL tabs, Job History and long hostnames** ([#8591]) - Job History on a PostgreSQL server now says the job history collector is written for SQL Server and never runs against another engine, instead of "No job runs recorded". The PostgreSQL Storage tab drops its repeated "estimates" wording and its development note, and shows byte counts in readable units. Column statistics round to three places, duration columns such as Backend Age use one format ("2m 5s", "1h 5m", "1d 8h 23m"), and the FinOps Index Analysis rollup rounds GB to two places. Long hostnames in the FinOps Server Inventory column, the FinOps Server box and the sidebar trim with an ellipsis (Lite gets the same trimming), and em dashes are gone from the PostgreSQL Vacuum headings, the Blocking summary and the Storage intro.
- **Darling reads no longer fail on a machine whose time separator is not a colon** ([#8607]) - Several Darling reads wrote their time cut-offs into the SQL with the machine's own time format. On a host set to a culture such as Danish, 02:00 came out as 02.00 and PostgreSQL rejected the statement. This hit the stitched rollup reads, the daily summary, the Server Inventory idle check and the idle-database recommendation. Those cut-offs are now always written in the fixed year-month-day hour:minute:second form. Output on other machines is unchanged.
- **PostgreSQL alert notebooks and triage pages show PostgreSQL reads for deadlocks, blocking and long-running queries** ([#8583], [#8594]) - The "Deadlocks Detected" and "Deadlocks Cleared" notebooks carried SQL Server cells only. The Blocking Detected, Blocking Wait Time and Long-Running Query notebooks, their Cleared notebooks and the triage pages for deadlock, blocking and long-running-query alerts listed SQL Server reads only. On a PostgreSQL target every cell said it did not apply or that the window had no data, even for an event the service had stored. These pages now pick their cells by the target's engine. On PostgreSQL they run the PostgreSQL deadlock, blocking and session reads (the deadlock list for the alert's window and the newest deadlock graphs) and no SQL Server read. A SQL Server target keeps the cells it had, and a server whose engine is not yet known shows both sets.
- **A read chart in an alert notebook draws the hours it read, and says which window that is** ([#8589]) - A read cell in an alert notebook asks for the window around its alert, but its time axis covered the last hours before the page was opened. Opened a day after a CPU Resolved alert, the chart showed an empty grid because the line sat outside the axis. The axis now ends at the time the read was pinned to, the cell shows "Pinned: start to end" with the date, and an empty answer says "in the 4 hours ending <time>" instead of "in the last 4 hours". A read with no pinned time, or one pinned to a time that has not passed yet, still ends now. Open live is unchanged.
- **Cleaner text in Lite and the Viewer after a click walk** ([#8622]) - Em dashes in text you read are now plain punctuation: the Azure sign-in choices (for example "Azure: Service Principal"), the Add Server dialogs, Settings, the Recommendations empty state, the Plan Corrections intro, status tooltips and window titles. The Collection Cycles line now says "all 41" when every collector is affected and tells you to open the Collection Log tab and set its time range back to those runs. FinOps > Index Analysis in Lite shows one empty message before a run instead of an empty grid above a message. The server tab header says "Connecting... waiting for the first collection", which is true whether or not stored data is already showing. FinOps > Index Analysis in the Viewer does the same: before data arrives, one message covers the page instead of an empty grid box with headers. Viewer labels, tooltips, status lines and message boxes use plain punctuation instead of em dashes.
- **analyze_server force-plan targets no longer tell you to wait for a revert that already happened** ([#8629]) - For a target whose automatic plan correction recommendation was already Reverted or Expired, the guidance said "intervene only if it reverts or expires". It now says the engine has backed out of it and a manual force is yours to decide, with the state, its reason and any blocker that still applies. The apc_owns_it blocker, here and in get_plan_force_precheck, now goes by the newer of the forced-plan observation and the automatic plan correction observation, so an older AUTO forced row no longer outranks a newer Reverted or Expired state, or a newer state that says the plan is not forced. findings_left_out entries now carry story_path and story_path_hash, so two left-out findings with the same headline can be told apart.
- **An alert notebook no longer calls its Server summary and Collection health cells "Pinned"** ([#8638]) - The web notebook for an alert such as Client Timeouts showed a yellow "Pinned: <alert window>" badge on both cells, but those two reads cannot be pinned and always showed the present. A read cell now draws the "Pinned" badge only when its read takes an `as_of`. A read that does not take one shows "Current state, not pinned" instead. Chart cells and reads such as CPU utilization keep their badge.
- **The web dashboard sidebar shows every page link when the window is tall enough** ([#8639]) - Since #8586 the page links sat in a box capped at 15rem (about six entries) at every window height, so most pages needed a scroll even on a 1080-high window. On a window with room for all sixteen links and five server rows, the links now take their own height with no inner scroll. On a shorter window the Views list gives up height first (it scrolls inside a smaller box), then the page links shrink with the window, down to the Pages button and five entries, so Alert History stays in view at 1280x720. The server list keeps at least five rows. The phone-width menu is unchanged.
- **Viewer Active Queries cells end in an ellipsis, and Server Inventory labels all its times** ([#8640]) - Login, Host, Program and the other text columns of the Active Queries grids, and the text columns of every Viewer grid that used the shared grid style, now end a long value with an ellipsis, with the full value in a tooltip on the Active Queries columns, instead of cutting it at the cell edge. Server Inventory's Start Time now follows the time display setting like Inventory As Of and Last Collected, and all three carry the server's offset in Server Time. The Start Time column was the server's own clock in every mode, and it is now converted to UTC or to this machine's time when those are chosen. Lite's Server Inventory Start Time is labelled the same way.
- **Empty charts look the same in Lite and the Viewer, and Duration Trends breaks at collection gaps** ([#8648]) - An empty chart now looks the same everywhere: one muted sentence, with no default axes or gridlines. This covers the Overview lanes, the System Events counter charts, and the CPU, Memory, Memory Grant, tempdb, File I/O, Collector Duration, Wait Stats, Memory Clerks and Perfmon charts. Duration Trends now breaks every line across a stretch where no collector ran, so a collector with only a few buckets no longer draws a line across hours with no data.
- **Lite and Viewer text and layout fixes from a click-through** ([#8646]) - The "New Child" button in Manage Query Tags now fits its label. Chart card descriptions, such as the 7-Day Provisioning Trend on FinOps > Utilization, wrap instead of ending in an ellipsis. In Server Time mode, Alert History's range and the Overview cards no longer take the zone of the last server tab you opened, and the Viewer's Alert History, Job History and Overview cards follow the same rule. The empty lists in Investigations, Manage Tags, Manage Query Tags and Add Multiple Servers now say what to do.
- **A server tab you just opened says "Loading..." until its first read returns** ([#8649]) - Sub-tabs of a server tab you just opened now say "Loading..." in their charts, grids, calendar and Collection Cycles card until their first read returns, instead of showing blank axes and empty grids. The "No data in this window" sentence now appears only after a read finds no rows. A sub-tab you pick while a new tab is still loading is read first. A chart with nothing ticked in the Perfmon, Wait Stats or Memory Clerks list now says "No counters picked", "No wait types picked" or "No clerks picked".
- **"Loading..." no longer stays on Top Queries by Duration, Top Procedures by Duration and Query Store by Duration** ([#8728]) - After the data loaded, a "Loading..." line stayed over the rows, or above the empty-state sentence. It came from the hidden comparison grid that sits under the main grid on these three tabs. That grid is never shown unless you turn on Compare, so nothing ever cleared it. A grid that is hidden no longer gets the line. The Darling Viewer has the same three tabs and gets the same fix.
- **Darling's AWS Performance Insights client no longer pulls in a package with a security advisory** ([#8652]) - The Darling service raised System.Formats.Cbor to 10.0.15, which fixes advisory GHSA-vmv3-wvjp-wqpj in a package the AWS Performance Insights client pulls in.
- **Job History's PostgreSQL message wraps to the width of the grid** ([#8669]) - In the Darling Viewer, the Job History tab drew its PostgreSQL message on one line that was wider than the grid, so the server name at the start and the closing sentence were cut off. It now wraps to the width of the grid.
- **The Job History time range picker uses your computer's time zone from the first open** ([#8669]) - In the Darling Viewer, the picker now words its range and its "Data starts" note in your computer's own time zone from the first time you open the tab. Before, it used a fixed offset until you opened a server tab, so a date in daylight saving time could show an hour early.
- **The PostgreSQL Storage page shows several rows of each grid** ([#8671]) - The five grids used to share the height the notes left over and showed one or two rows each. Each grid now has room for about eight rows, and the page scrolls when they do not all fit on one screen.
- **PostgreSQL tab text is cleaner** ([#8671]) - The Overview collectors note no longer shows an issue number, the logging settings grid names the Viewer's own tabs instead of MCP tool and field names, "1 transactions" now reads "1 transaction" in the Vacuum session grid, and the Vacuum grids end a cut value with an ellipsis and show the whole value in a tooltip.
- **The monitor's own PostgreSQL statements no longer carry long development notes** ([#8670]) - The statement-text fetch and the statements of the other PostgreSQL collectors, 61 comments in all, had their explanations written inside the SQL itself. That text went to every monitored server with each run, was kept in the server's statement statistics, and filled the monitor's own row in Query Shapes. The explanations now sit in C# comments beside the SQL. The SQL that runs is the same apart from the removed comments, and the monitor's own statements are still recognised by the short marker at the end of each one.
- **find_queries_by_text answers not_collected on a PostgreSQL server** ([#8685]) - On a PostgreSQL server it no longer answers "empty" for the Query Store and plan-cache sources. Both sources read SQL Server tables that a PostgreSQL server never fills, so each now answers not_collected, names the engine and points at get_pg_top_queries, which holds PostgreSQL statement text. Neither read runs. SQL Server servers are unchanged.
- **A get_fleet_overview cards page is worst first all the way down** ([#8667]) - The byte-budget page ordered only the five servers in worst_servers and left every other card in server-name order, so a Healthy card could be returned while Critical ones were left out. Every card is now placed by the ranking worst_servers uses, and cards_left_out and the later pages follow the same order.
- **An idle Fleet Overview reads each store's fleet roll-up once per refresh, not twice** ([#8636]) - Behind a front door with three stores the page read every store's `/api/fleet` twice a minute, once for the sidebar and again about two seconds later for the page, about 0.67 MB a minute per open tab. The page's refresh now takes the roll-up the sidebar just read. Navigating to a page, changing a picker or range and a refresh that starts later than 20 s after the sidebar's still read their own, and a failed sidebar read is not reused. A server Overview and a saved view share the same read.
- **The web dashboard's own reads no longer wait behind the Queries tab's slow reads** ([#8635], [#8686]) - On a server's first Queries visit, five slow tab reads (the three duration trends and the two top lists) held five of the browser's six connections, so the status bar, the store host and the alert badge waited for a connection and showed late. Those five now share the page's read gate with the four Query Store reads, under one limit of four reads at once. The active-query snapshots and the Query Store top list were the only two reads of the tab not held to that limit, and they now share it too, so the page's own reads get a connection at once. The tab settles a couple of seconds later than before on a cold first visit.
- **A slow first open in the Viewer now names the slow read** ([#8602], [#8642], [#8703]) - The slow-load log line for Overview, Queries, Daily Summary, Collection Health, File I/O, Perfmon and Latches and Spinlocks said no store read was timed and blamed client work, and Agent Alerts, Alert History and FinOps wrote no line. Each read is now timed by name, the grid and chart builds are listed as client steps, and the fleet tabs log slow loads too. A server tab's first open now runs on one clock from the moment the tab is shown to its first status stamp. An open over three seconds logs one `[SlowLoad]` line naming the server's display name, each store read with its milliseconds, and the UI-thread steps, including the reads before the landing sub-tab (the server clock, data start, freshness, permission count and collection interval) and the wait behind another tab's refresh. FinOps Utilization names each of its reads and the layout pass after it, and the other FinOps sub-tabs name their reads in that line too. Alert History's first open is timed from the moment the tab is shown. Utilization, Locking, Database Sizes and Version Store now run their independent reads side by side.
- **The status bar says the picked time range is loading, and no longer says "refreshed" over the old range's data** ([#8602]) - A slow Viewer tab (Query Store by Duration over 30 days) kept the previous range's grid and slicer on screen while the header already named the new range, and a pick made during a refresh was read only after that refresh stamped "refreshed" for the old window. The status bar now says "loading" with the range's name from the pick, and a load with another refresh queued behind it leaves the stamp to the queued one.
- **A 30-day Daily Summary read no longer re-reads a month the service has already cached** ([#8602]) - The service warmed a 31-day block and a call asking for 30 days used a different cache key, so it read the whole month cold. A shorter range that ends the same day now starts from the longer block's closed days and reads only the open days. The Viewer's own first open is not changed.
- **A server tab opened while the Overview is still loading no longer waits for it** ([#8693]) - The Viewer used one slot for every "refresh the tab on screen" request, so a server tab opened during the Overview's first read (or its timer's refresh) queued behind it. Fleet tabs and server tabs now have a slot each, and the tabs inside one slot still run one at a time.
- **The Top Queries by Duration grid reads a busy day window much faster** ([#8693]) - The read ranked every group of the window and built each group's full row in the same pass, which spilled hundreds of megabytes to disk on a busy store. It now ranks on the group key and the sum first, then builds the full row for the kept groups only. On a test store of 3,168,000 rows in a 24-hour window the Viewer's read went from 13.3 s to 2.3 s warm, with the same rows. Lite's Top Queries read made the same change, and on the same row count it went from 5.2 to 8.6 s down to about 0.9 s.
- **Top Queries by Duration finds each statement's module without sorting or scanning the whole procedure history** ([#8602], [#8674], [#8677]) - The Viewer named each statement's stored procedure by ranking every procedure_stats row of the ranked handles, and the cost grew with retention. It now looks for each handle's newest row among the rows since the start of the window first. Only a statement with no recent row, such as an ad hoc batch, makes the read go back through older history, and then it reads that history once. Lite's read takes the newest row with one aggregate and no sort, and on a test table of 3.5 million rows the lookup went from 295 ms to 64 ms. The module shown is the same, and when two rows of one handle tie on the newest collection time it is the row collected last.
- **Lite's memory trim works once its indexes are large, runs when a server tab closes, and the log shows where the memory is** ([#8624], [#8643], [#8673]) - The trim that gives DuckDB's cache back to the operating system lowers `memory_limit` to 64 MB, which DuckDB refuses once its indexes hold more than that, so the trim failed every minute and only wrote a debug line. The target now sits above what DuckDB cannot evict, and a refused trim is logged. The trim starts only when the cache it can free is over 256 MB, so a store whose indexes alone are that large no longer trims every minute and reads its data again. Closing a server tab now asks for the same trim at once, so the memory the tab read comes back right away instead of at the next one-minute tick. If a tab load or a collection is using the local database, the close does not wait and the next tick takes the trim. Lite also writes one log line when a server tab opens and when it closes, and one for each trim tick that trimmed, was skipped because the local database was busy or was refused. Each of those lines gives DuckDB's memory by tag next to the process's private bytes and the gc heap. The open and close line is retried for up to 30 seconds while the local database is busy. If it is still busy, the line is written with the process figures and a note that the DuckDB figures were not read.
- **The 30-day Daily Summary reads a daily wait totals rollup instead of a month of raw waits** ([#8620]) - The wait part of the Daily Summary grouped every raw `wait_stats` row in the window, the largest share of the statement on a store with a month of data. Store migration V259 adds `collect.wait_stats_daily`, a TimescaleDB continuous aggregate (a plain view on a store without TimescaleDB) holding the summed wait time for each server, UTC day and wait type, kept 35 days. The start-up sweep gives it a refresh policy that runs hourly over four days, so yesterday is in the rollup after about 01:00 UTC, and fills the closed days once, in the background. The Daily Summary, the Viewer and the `get_daily_summary` and `get_daily_summary_range` tools read the closed days from it and read raw rows for the open day, any day the rollup does not hold and any window edge that is not midnight. Where the relation is missing, the read is unchanged. Raw waits are kept 30 days, so a day older than 30 already showed its queries and no waits. The rollup keeps 35 days, so the 5 days after raw drops them now show their waits. An older build ignores the rollup, so nothing needs doing before a rollback.
- **The Daily Summary reads collector run counts from the hourly rollup for the whole month** ([#8620], [#8704]) - The per-day collector runs and errors were counted over the raw collection log, which on a busy store meant reading every uncompressed chunk. Closed hours now come from the hourly rollup, and the raw log is read only for the hours it does not hold, so the counts are unchanged, including while the rollup is behind. The service now keeps 31 days and 3 hours of that rollup where it kept 7 days, so a 31-day read no longer goes back to the raw log for the older days. After an upgrade it fills the extra days at one day per hourly pass, so the month read gets faster over the first 24 hours. The 7-day health read still reads only its own 7 days. The rollup table grows about four times larger, roughly 12 MB for each monitored server. Lite has no such rollup and is unchanged.
- **The Daily Summary's unique-query count reads closed days from the rollups instead of raw query_stats** ([#8620], [#8702]) - The month read counted unique queries for every day by sorting every hourly rollup row of the month, which spilled to disk on a busy server, and the 30-day read went to raw query_stats for yesterday because the daily rollup leaves yesterday out until the next UTC midnight. It now counts each closed day from the daily query rollup, which holds about 24 times fewer rows. A day the daily rollup lacks reads the hourly rollup for the hours it holds, and raw rows are read only for the hours neither rollup holds and for the open day. A store without the daily rollup reads as before. Lite has no rollup tables and is unchanged.
- **The daily rollup backfill at start-up waits for a running refresh and no longer does thousands of empty refreshes** ([#8684]) - On a large store, the refresh policy that the upgrade creates is still running when the service starts, and the backfill gave up on the first "concurrent refresh" error. That left most of the 35 days out of `wait_stats_daily` until the next restart, so the Daily Summary read them from raw data, which is exact but slow. The backfill now retries the same slice every 15 seconds for up to 10 minutes. Separately, a store with no `job_history` rows made the backfill run about 5,200 seven-day refreshes on every start. The backfill now does nothing when the raw table is empty.
- **The Viewer's Query Store grid reads a long window once and sorts less** ([#8692], [#8695]) - On a window of 12 hours or more the grid read every interval of the window a second time to fetch the rows of the queries it kept. On a large store that doubled the disk reads of the first open and could push the read past its deadline, so it started over on the raw history. The grid now fetches the kept queries' rows through an index. The raw history read no longer sorts every row at full width. It ranks on a narrow dedupe and fetches the full rows of the kept groups only, which cut temp file writes about 3.5 times on a seeded store. When the interval-table read fails and the read goes on to raw history, the Viewer log now gets one Warning line naming the read, the time spent and the reason. The grid shows the same rows.
- **The Viewer's Query Store tab opens faster the first time at Past 30 days** ([#8705]) - On a large store the first read of the 30-day interval table could run just past the Viewer's 15 second wait, so the Viewer gave up and read the slower raw history instead.
