The grid and the duration strip now wait up to 25 seconds for the interval table before they fall back to raw history. The duration strip's table read also does less sorting. It adds up the bucket totals directly and counts distinct queries in a separate narrow step, so on a test table of 2.7 million interval rows it writes about 85 percent fewer temporary blocks. The bars show the same numbers. No other read's deadline changed.
- **Past 30 days Query Store reads in the Viewer read far fewer pages on a store that has run for a few days** ([#8707]) - The grid ranking step and the slicer read tens of thousands of heap pages per day for one server, because every server's rows share the same pages. The hourly maintenance pass now builds a covering index on each day partition that the collector can no longer update, one partition at a time and without blocking writes. On a test store with 8 servers and 11 days, the ranking step read 27,875 pages instead of 138,468 and the slicer 27,539 instead of 138,468. The pass also vacuums each of those partitions that still has rows added since its last vacuum, because autovacuum leaves the newest pages of a closed partition unmarked and the index scan would otherwise go back to the table for about 15 percent of one server's rows there. On a test store that cut the reads of each closed partition from 1,455 pages to 301. Today's partition and the days the collector can still reach are left alone, so the collector's updates stay heap-only. Reads name no index, so a partition without it reads as before. Lite is not affected.
- **The Job History "Data starts" note reads a daily rollup instead of sorting the job history** ([#8679]) - The note under the time range picker no longer sorts every compressed chunk of the job history table to find each server's first run. A new store migration (V263) adds a small daily rollup of the oldest run per server, `collect.job_history_oldest_daily`. The note reads the closed days from the rollup and only the oldest day of the table and the open day from the raw rows, so the answer is the same as before. A store without TimescaleDB keeps the old one-row reads. The rollup is filled once at the next service start and trimmed with the raw job history by the daily purge.
- **The Job History grid and the MCP job history tools load faster on a long window** ([#8679]) - A 30-day window of the all-servers read was slow, because it decompressed and sorted every compressed chunk once per server to find each server's newest rows. For a window of 10 days or longer with no end date, the read now looks at the last two days of collections first and keeps that answer for each server that filled its row cap there, provably the same rows. A server that did not (a quiet one, or one with a late batch of old runs) still takes the full read. On a test store of 4.9 million rows the part that changed went from 2,471 ms to 317 ms at 30 days. Windows under 10 days read as before.
- **The Job History Category list opens fast on a busy store** ([#8694], [#8706]) - The read that fills the Category drop-down is the slowest statement the tab runs, because on a store with many monitored servers it read every job run in the time range to find a few category names. The service now keeps a small hourly summary of the job categories (new store version V265, two small tables that start empty) and the list reads that instead, plus the few runs collected since the last pass. The first passes after the upgrade fill the summary one day at a time, newest first, and until the summary covers a range the list reads job runs as before. The categories offered are the same. A build older than V265 ignores the tables. The Viewer and Lite also keep each answer for 30 minutes per window and server, and loads that overlap share one read, where the Viewer used to start the read again on every refresh, on every window change and whenever a newer load replaced the one waiting on it. A category that first appears after the read still reaches the drop-down, because the drop-down also adds the categories of the runs the grid loads.
- **`get_oversized_plan_backlog` reads the backlog table once for its per-collector counts, not twice** ([#8570]) - The fleet form of the read scanned the whole backlog table twice (once for its counts, once to count the servers), so the first call after a restart paid two full reads of a table that can be large on a busy store. It now counts the servers from the same pass that counts the rows. The answer is unchanged.
- **MCP reply text is plainer and its windows are true** ([#8572]) - Daily report details, alert texts, tool notes and PostgreSQL advice no longer carry em dashes, spaced hyphens used as dashes, issue numbers or migration numbers. Large millisecond and microsecond figures are grouped (6,631.1 ms, 466,737 us). The slow-read statement label starts at the statement, not at its leading comment. An empty-window reply for a call with as_of names the window it looked at ("in the 24 hours ending at ..."). Tool descriptions say what a setting does instead of which migration added it. Service log lines are unchanged.
- **A server's page starts its reads at once when a database filter is saved in the browser** ([#8554]) - With a saved database filter on any server, the Overview of every server held all its reads until the fleet roll-up of its store answered, which can take a second or more on a large store. The browser now remembers each server's id, so a server's reads leave straight away with its own filter (or none) applied. A server this browser has never opened still waits for its own store's roll-up, and PostgreSQL servers still wait for the card that names their engine.
- **The server inventory plans the size history once, not three times** ([#8570]) - The inventory fleet read looked up each server's newest database-size snapshot three times, and each lookup planned every chunk of the size history. A first call on a busy store paid for that in catalog reads before it started. The newest snapshot is now read once, and its rows feed both the storage totals and the idle database check, so the plan names each chunk once. The figures and the idle database counts are unchanged.
- **The plan regression read no longer sorts every plan to pick two plan ids** ([#8570]) - For every hash group in the window, the plan regression read picked the newest and the most-executed plan id with two ordered aggregates, which ran a separate sort per group to keep 100 rows. On a busy store that is many thousands of groups. The two picks now run over the 100 candidates only, with the same ordering and tie-breaks, so the rows are unchanged and the read does less work.
- **The Viewer's Query Store banner names the slicer's real start, and a server tab refresh loads its sub-tab without waiting for the header's reads** ([#8549]) - Over a window of seven days or more, the interval table draws the Query Store slicer, but the banner named the raw table's floor as "slicer since". It now names the start of the source that drew the slicer, on a range load and on a slicer drag, and a dragged range the interval table serves gets that table's start for the grid. A sub-tab pick, a range change and the auto-refresh tick no longer wait for the freshness readout and the permission-denied count before the sub-tab's own reads begin. Both now run beside the load. The slow-load line also counts the reads that still run before the load, and the wait behind a refresh already in flight, so its total matches the time to the "refreshed" stamp.
- **`get_query_fleet_summary` reads whole days of its Query Store source from the daily rollup** ([#8570]) - On a busy store the first call after a restart spent many seconds unpacking hourly rollup chunks to find one statement's rows. Closed days that have settled now come from the daily rollup, which holds 24 times fewer rows. The hourly rollup is read only for the first and last partial days and for the days the daily rollup can still change. The answer is unchanged. A store without a daily rollup, or with a day missing from it, reads as before.
- **Query-tag sightings no longer rewrite every index on each re-sighting** ([#8716]) - The query-tag sightings table moved its indexed last-seen time on every re-sighting, so no update was HOT, every update wrote all four indexes, and autovacuum ran many times a day on a busy store while removing no pages. A re-sighting now adds to its count and moves the last-seen time at most once an hour (or when it fills in an object name or text sample the row lacked), and the table's fillfactor is set to 80 (schema version 266) so new pages have room for HOT updates. In a test with 1.2 million re-sightings, HOT updates went from 0% to 77% and WAL fell by more than half. The fillfactor applies to new pages only, so an existing table keeps its fill until it is rewritten. Lite's sightings table moves its last-seen time the same way. The tag map is unchanged: a new key or a new caller combination still takes the current time at once.
- **The Viewer's Job History tab now logs slow loads** ([#8549]) - The tab wrote no SlowLoad line, so a slow first open or a slow Category list read left no trace in the Viewer log. A load or first open over three seconds now writes one line that names the read that was slow (the grid, the Category list, the server list, the data-start probe) and the client steps, the same as the other tabs.
- **The attention table purge finds its oldest expired row through the server index** ([#8719]) - On a large store the daily purge of the client timeout events found its oldest expired row with two scans that, once earlier purges had left pages not yet vacuumed, fetched most of the table's heap pages, so the delete read the table three times instead of once. The oldest expired time is now found through the server index in a few hundred buffer reads. The same rows are deleted.
- **The Overview card words an empty Last Collect on the same clock it uses for its status** ([#8722]) - The card decided "None retained" or "Never" from the wall clock, while its status band used the time it was given. The two sometimes disagreed about whether a server's history had aged out. The wording now reads the same time as the band.
- **Job History loads faster on large stores and a quick re-pick no longer stacks reads** ([#8549]) - The Job History grid looked up each server's UTC offset by scanning every chunk of the server properties history, and it now reads only the newest offset for each server it shows (the same answer, about 26 times fewer pages for that step on a test store). A load that a newer pick supersedes now cancels its grid and status reads instead of leaving them running. The Category list's hourly summary now catches up four days an hour instead of one, one day at a time within a 30 second budget, so after an upgrade a 30-day Category list reads the summary instead of the raw history after about 8 hours instead of about 30, and a very large store still moves forward every hour.
- **MCP tool guides send an agent only to tools the connected server serves** ([#8252]) - Six tool guides, two parameter descriptions and one refusal message pointed an agent at a tool its server does not have, so an agent that followed them got an unknown-tool error. In Darling, `recompile_module` and `evict_cached_plan` named `get_procedure_stats`, which neither product has. They now name `get_top_procedures_by_cpu` and its `full_name`, or `get_top_queries_by_cpu` for a `plan_handle`. The `analyze_server`, `get_collection_health` and `get_query_store_health` guides in Lite now say which pointers are for Darling only. The `get_plan_corrections` guide compares its `full_text` opt-in to `get_query_store_top`, which both products serve.
- **MCP reply text: three more small fixes** ([#8738]) - get_pg_column_stats rounds correlation, top value frequency and null fraction to six significant digits, get_ag_health ends a cut list of availability groups with "and N more" instead of an ellipsis, and the get_long_query_completions description now says it answers not_collected when the collector has never run for a server.
- **A Job History load stopped by a newer pick now names the read it was waiting on** ([#8549]) - the slow-load log line for a load that a newer pick replaced left out the read still running and counted its whole time as client work. The line now lists that read with its elapsed time and "(stopped by a newer pick)", and client work counts only real client time.
- **A web server page opened by part of a server name now opens that server** ([#8741]) - a link with part of a server's name drew every panel but showed the server as unknown in the header. The page now finds the one server the name matches and opens it by its own name. A name that matches no server, or more than one, shows a "no monitored server" message instead of panels.
- **The plan advisory read checks each plan once instead of once per snapshot** ([#8743]) - the analysis pass looked up the plan table once for every snapshot row of every plan in the window before it kept the ten costliest. It now looks up each distinct plan once, so the read does a small fraction of the index lookups on a large store. The ten plans it returns are unchanged.
